## Active Task
None — the assistant just implemented floor‑hue segmentation crops and mobile UI improvements, redeployed, and is awaiting user feedback.

## Goal
Build a mobile‑first book‑sorting MVP that allows DJ’s wife to review floor photos of kids' books (coloring, activity, sticker), mark each as keep or discard, and export keep/discard lists. Host it privately on Railway with a secret link, as a dedicated service separate from HealthOS.

## Constraints & Preferences
- **Deploy target:** Railway (chief‑cloud project) — but NOT inside HealthOS; must be its own service/repo.
- **Access:** private, secret‑link (no public browsing).
- **Photo handling:** upload one or more wide floor photos; no need to store original images.
- **Detection:** best‑effort automatic detection, with a manual review step. Duplicates share the same decision.
- **UI:** mobile‑first, photo‑gallery grid (3×3 or responsive), green check / red X corner buttons, tap centre to enlarge. Category auto‑suggested. Tapping a duplicate toggles all copies.
- **Output:** keep list, discard list. Cards show: red X = discard, green check = keep, blank = undecided.
- **Code management:** must use GitHub; no editing in ephemeral VM filesystem alone; remove local scratch files after deployment.
- **Separation of projects:** Book Sorter must be a separate GitHub repo and a separate Railway service, distinct from HealthOS.
- **Cover rotation:** User expects each book’s cover text to be readable upright; manual per‑book rotation controls are now available.
- **Mobile layout:** Header must be compact; intro paragraph and hint box hidden on small screens; summary row hidden on mobile.
- **Decision buttons:** green check and red X should be transparent with colored outlines, not solid filled circles.

## Completed Actions
1. Scoped the MVP with DJ, confirmed “automatic detection + manual review” approach, mobile‑first gallery, green/red corner buttons.
2. Wrote the MVP static assets inside the scratch Hermes tree:
   - `hermes_cli/assets/books_sorter.html` (self‑contained mobile gallery, JS logic for detection, marking, duplicate grouping, category suggestions, JSON/text export)

You’re right: that approach is materially better than what I was doing.

I replaced the app’s sample-set pipeline with a floor-hue segmentation crop generator based on the method you pasted.

What changed:
- Added a Python crop generator using HSV floor masking:
  - detects the brown floor by hue
  - inverts to book mask
  - applies morphology
  - splits merged blobs by valley cuts
  - tightens boxes to book-mask pixels
  - rejects low-fill / floor-like crops
- Generated static crop assets under:
  - `sample-crops/`
  - `sample-crops/manifest.json`
- Mounted `/sample-crops` in the FastAPI app.
- Changed the app so “Load sample set” now loads the generated crop manifest instead of my brittle hand-box specs.
- Kept the mobile UI fixes:
  - intro hidden on mobile
  - sticky header removed on mobile
  - hint box hidden on mobile
  - transparent outlined green/red action buttons

Live verification passed:
- Manifest loaded from production.
- Generated crop count: **70**
- First generated crop served as JPEG from production.
- Mobile compact CSS present.
- Transparent outlined buttons present.

Live:
`https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c`

This does **not** solve orientation automatically — the method you pasted is correct that cropping and orientation are separate. But this should be a much more sane cropping foundation.

- **Check/X buttons are circles again**
  - Fixed the CSS so they have fixed width/height, `aspect-ratio: 1 / 1`, no flex stretching, and `border-radius: 50%`.

- **Removed the rotate button from the top/gallery image cards**
  - Rotation now remains available only in the detailed modal view.

- **Restored default orientation work**
  - The new floor-hue crops had reset every rotation to `0`.
  - I applied the previous rotation/title work back onto the generated crop manifest.
  - Also removed one bad wood-only crop, so the sample set is now **69 crops**.

- **Improved detail modal**
  - Modal title now uses the deciphered book title when available.
  - If there is no usable title, it shows **Untitled**.
  - The subtitle now says only:
    - `Category: Activity`
    - `Category: Coloring`
    - `Category: Sticker`
  - Removed the noisy `undecided · rotate 0° · hash ...` text.
  - Added green check and red X buttons over the modal image itself, matching the gallery behavior.

- **Verified**
  - Local regression tests pass: **7/7**.
  - Production verification passed:
    - modal image check/X controls present
    - circular button CSS present
    - gallery rotate button removed
    - category subtitle present
    - hash/rotation debug subtitle removed
    - manifest has **69** items
    - titles and rotations are live
    - bad `01-13` crop removed

Live URL:
`https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c`

### assistant

```text
Yes. On every page reload, the app currently:

- Loads the bundled crop manifest from `/sample-crops/manifest.json`
- Creates the individual book cards from those cropped images
- Then reapplies any saved decisions/rotations from your browser’s `localStorage`

So it is **not re-running detection from the original floor photos** each time. It is loading the already-generated individual book crop files.

Important detail:
- Your keep/discard/rotation choices are saved locally in that browser/device.
- If you reload the same browser, they should come back.
- If you open the link on another phone/browser, those decisions won’t be there unless we add server-side saving or an import/export flow.
```

{"path":"/opt/data/projects/book-sorter-mvp/tests/test_server_decisions.py","content":"import importlib\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nimport unittest\n\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\n\n\nclass ServerDecisionTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def setUp(self):\n        self.tmp = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()\n        os.environ[\"BOOK_SORTER_DATA_DIR\"] = self.tmp.name\n        import app\n        self.app_module = importlib.reload(app)\n        self.client = TestClient(self.app_module.app)\n\n    def tearDown(self):\n        self.tmp.cleanup()\n        os.environ.pop(\"BOOK_SORTER_DATA_DIR\", None)\n\n    def test_decisions_are_saved_and_loaded_server_side(self):\n        initial = self.client.get(\"/api/decisions\")\n        self.assertEqual(initial.status_code, 200)\n        self.assertEqual(initial.json()[\"decisions\"], {})\n\n        saved = self.client.patch(\n            \"/api/decisions/abc123\",\n            json={\"category\": \"activity\", \"decision\": \"keep\", \"rotation\": 90},\n        )\n        self.assertEqual(saved.status_code, 200)\n        self.assertTrue(saved.json()[\"ok\"])\n\n        loaded = self.client.get(\"/api/decisions\")\n        self.assertEqual(loaded.status_code, 200)\n        record = loaded.json()[\"decisions\"][\"abc123\"]\n        self.assertEqual(record[\"category\"], \"activity\")\n        self.assertEqual(record[\"decision\"], \"keep\")\n        self.assertEqual(record[\"rotation\"], 90)\n\n    def test_clear_decisions(self):\n        self.client.patch(\"/api/decisions/abc123\", json={\"category\": \"activity\", \"decision\": \"discard\", \"rotation\": 0})\n        cleared = self.client.delete(\"/api/decisions\")\n        self.assertEqual(cleared.status_code, 200)\n        self.assertEqual(self.client.get(\"/api/decisions\").json()[\"decisions\"], {})\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    unittest.main()\n"}

{"success": true, "name": "railway-app-deployment", "description": "Deploy web apps to Railway: CLI auth, project/service setup, Postgres, env vars, health checks, migrations, and verification.", "tags": ["railway", "deployment", "postgres", "nextjs", "prisma", "auth", "devops"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-repo-management", "cron-job-design"], "content": "---\nname: railway-app-deployment\ndescription: \"Deploy web apps to Railway: CLI auth, project/service setup, Postgres, env vars, health checks, migrations, and verification.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [railway, deployment, postgres, nextjs, prisma, auth, devops]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-repo-management, cron-job-design]\n---\n\n# Railway App Deployment\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse this skill when deploying or troubleshooting a web app on Railway, especially:\n\n- creating/linking Railway projects or services\n- provisioning PostgreSQL\n- setting app environment variables\n- deploying Next.js/Node apps\n- running migrations/seeds after deploy\n- verifying health checks\n- setting up cron/backup jobs tied to Railway-hosted apps\n\n## Core Workflow\n\nWhen the user says the app is a *separate project*, treat that as a cue to build and deploy it as its own repo/service instead of embedding it in an unrelated product's codebase. Keep product boundaries explicit: separate app, separate repo, separate Railway service, separate verification URL.\n\nSee `references/standalone-services-and-repo-connection.md` for the repo/service split and connection flow.\nSee `references/service-domain-discovery.md` for live-domain lookup and GitHub connection GraphQL snippets.\n\n1. **Verify local build first**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   npm run test\n   DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/app SESSION_SECRET=dev-secret npm run typecheck\n   DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/app SESSION_SECRET=dev-secret npm run build\n   git status --short\n   ``​`\n\n2. **Authenticate Railway**\n\n   Check status:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway whoami 2>&1 || true\n   ``​`\n\n   Preferred for automation/headless environments:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   export RAILWAY_API_TOKEN='<token>'\n   railway whoami\n   ``​`\n\n   Some older docs/scripts use `RAILWAY_TOKEN`, but CLI v5 expects `RAILWAY_API_TOKEN` for token auth. Normalize user-provided token names this way:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   export RAILWAY_API_TOKEN=\"${RAILWAY_API_TOKEN:-${RAILWAY_TOKEN:-}}\"\n   ``​`\n\n   In headless or chat-driven sessions, prefer env-var auth over interactive login flows. `railway login` only offers browserless auth in this CLI, so token-based automation should be done by exporting `RAILWAY_API_TOKEN` (or `RAILWAY_TOKEN` for older scripts) before running CLI commands.\n\n   Verify auth immediately before any Railway API or deploy action:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway whoami\n   ``​`\n\n   If `whoami` fails, fix the token first; do not spend time probing repo links or GraphQL mutations until auth is valid.\n\n   For headless or non-interactive sessions, prefer a persisted token in `/opt/data/.env` as `RAILWAY_API_TOKEN` (or `RAILWAY_TOKEN` for older scripts). In this environment, the Railway CLI can still report `Unauthorized` even when the token is valid, so treat direct GraphQL reads as the source of truth when CLI auth is flaky.\n\n   If `whoami` is inconclusive but you have a fresh token, do a direct GraphQL read against `https://backboard.railway.com/graphql/v2` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and a browser-like `User-Agent` to confirm the token actually works for API reads. See `references/railway-auth-verification.md`.\n\n   Common GraphQL operations for automation:\n   - `serviceConnect` — attach a GitHub repo to a Railway service\n   - `serviceInstanceAutoDeployStatus` — confirm auto-deploy is enabled\n   - `serviceDomainCreate` — create the default Railway domain when `domains` is empty\n   - `serviceInstanceDeployV2` — trigger a deploy from a specific Git commit SHA\n   - `deployments` — list/poll deployment status and build IDs\n\n3. **Avoid repeated browserless auth loops**\n\n   `railway login --browserless` may require an interactive terminal and can expire if the URL/code is not approved promptly. In bridged/headless contexts, try browserless once; if it expires or only shows a spinner, switch to token auth. Do not keep spawning stale login waiters.\n\n   Cleanup:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   pkill -f 'railway login' || true\n   ps -ef | grep -E 'railway login' | grep -v grep || true\n   railway whoami 2>&1 || true\n   ``​`\n\n4. **Create/link project and service**\n\n   Typical commands vary by Railway CLI version; check help first:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway --help\n   railway init --help\n   railway link --help\n   ``​`\n\n   Common flow:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway init\n   railway link\n   ``​`\n\n   Before pushing anything, confirm whether you are working in the *deployable app repo* or a *runtime/state backup repo*. If the repo role is unclear, inspect the remote, the project docs, and the Railway connection before assuming a push will affect production.\n\n5. **Provision PostgreSQL**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway add --database postgres\n   railway variables\n   ``​`\n\n   Confirm `DATABASE_URL` is available to the app service.\n\n6. **Set required env vars**\n\n   Example for a Next.js/Prisma app:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway variables set NODE_ENV=production\n   railway variables set SESSION_SECRET='<long-random-secret>'\n   railway variables set GOOGLE_SHEETS_TOKEN_PATH=/opt/data/google-accounts/personal/google_token.json\n   railway variables set GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET_PATH=/opt/data/google_client_secret.json\n   ``​`\n\n   Do not print secrets back to the user.\n\n7. **Deploy**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway up\n   ``​`\n\n   For GraphQL-triggered deploys, use the current `serviceInstanceDeployV2(serviceId:, environmentId:, commitSha:)` signature and pass the exact Git SHA after pushi

### assistant

```text
Done and deployed.

What changed:

- Added **server-side shared saving**:
  - Keep/discard/category/rotation decisions now save to the Railway app server.
  - Both you and your wife will load the same shared decisions from `/api/decisions`.
  - Changes are saved immediately when a decision/category/rotation changes.
  - The app also refreshes shared decisions every ~10 seconds, so another device’s changes can show up without a full reload.

- Added a **persistent Railway volume**:
  - Mounted at `/data`
  - App stores shared decisions at `/data/book-sorter/decisions.json`
  - This means the decisions should survive normal app restarts/redeploys, not just live in memory.

- Kept local browser storage as a fallback:
  - If the server save/load ever fails, the browser still has localStorage.

- Also finished the previous interrupted detail-view request:
  - Tapping the large center ✓ / ✕ overlay now clears the decision.

Verified:
- Local tests pass:
  - UI/static tests: **9/9**
  - server decision API tests: **2/2**
- Railway volume is live:
  - `book-sorter-volume /data READY`
- Production verified:
  - shared decision API loads
  - shared decision save works
  - app HTML includes server load/save/refresh logic
  - center overlay clear handler is live

Live:
`https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/books/4f8b2d7c`
```

### user

```text
[DJ Mauch] Is there an easy way for me to see whether other people are interacting with the website? I’m specifically curious to see when my wife access is it and how much time she spends on it
```

### assistant

```text
Yes — easiest is to add a tiny built-in activity tracker to this app.

I’d recommend this version:

- When someone opens the page, the app creates a browser/device session ID.
- It sends a small heartbeat to the server every ~15–30 seconds while the page is visible.
- The server records:
  - first visit time
  - last active time
  - total active time
  - page loads
  - number of keep/discard/category/rotation actions
  - rough device label, e.g. “iPhone Safari”
- Add a private admin page like:
  - `/admin/activity/<secret>`
- The admin page would show:
  - “Active now”
  - “Last seen”
  - “Total time today”
  - “Books marked keep/discard”
  - per-device/session summary

Best way to know it’s your wife specifically:
- Add a very small “Who is this?” prompt the first time:
  - `DJ`
  - `Wife`
- Or create separate links:
  - DJ link
  - Wife link

Without one of those, we can only infer from device/browser.

Important caveat: if you want this to be transparent, we can put a small note on the page like “Activity is saved so decisions sync across devices.” Since this is a family/private tool, that’s probably enough.

Technically this is straightforward now that server-side saving is already in place. We can store activity in the same Railway volume alongside the shared decisions.
```

Done and deployed.

I added activity tracking for the site.

You can view it here:

`https://book-sorter-production.up.railway.app/admin/activity/4f8b2d7c`

It now tracks:

- Browser/device sessions
- First seen
- Last seen
- Active now / last active
- Approximate active time
- Page loads
- Keep/discard/clear/category/rotation action counts
- Last action
- Rough device label, e.g. iPhone / Mac / Android
- User agent string

The client now sends:

- A heartbeat on page load
- A heartbeat every 15 seconds while the page is open/visible
- A heartbeat on visibility change
- An action event when someone marks keep/discard, clears, changes category, or rotates

Storage:
- Activity is stored server-side in the same Railway persistent volume as shared decisions.

Verified:
- Local tests pass:
  - UI/static tests: **11/11**
  - decision API tests: **2/2**
  - activity API/admin tests: **3/3**
- Deployed to Railway successfully.
- Production verified:
  - app includes heartbeat/action tracking
  - admin activity page loads
  - production heartbeat write works

  - activity data is persisted and visible via the API/admin page.

Fetch the live app route and confirm it returns HTTP 200.\n2. Confirm the page contains a unique app marker, such as the app title or a known UI string.\n3. Fetch at least one linked asset or sub-route and confirm the MIME type or payload is correct.\n4. If the app is framed by a wrapper page, verify the wrapper points to the embedded app source and the source contains the expected title/marker.\n\n## Useful markers\n- app title or hero text\n- iframe `src` for wrapper pages\n- asset `Content-Type` such as `image/jpeg` or `text/html`\n- health endpoint JSON like `{\"ok\":true}`\n", "file_type": ".md"}

```text
{"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent-skill-authoring", "description": "Author in-repo SKILL.md: frontmatter, validator, structure.", "tags": ["skills", "authoring", "hermes-agent", "conventions", "skill-md"], "related_skills": ["writing-plans", "requesting-code-review"], "content": "---\nname: hermes-agent-skill-authoring\ndescription: \"Author in-repo SKILL.md: frontmatter, validator, structure.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [skills, authoring, hermes-agent, conventions, skill-md]\n    related_skills: [writing-plans, requesting-code-review]\n---\n\n# Authoring Hermes-Agent Skills (in-repo)\n\n## Overview\n\nThere are two places a SKILL.md can live:\n\n1. **User-local:** `~/.hermes/skills/<maybe-category>/<name>/SKILL.md` — personal, not shared. Created via `skill_manage(action='create')`.\n2. **In-repo (this skill is about this case):** `/home/bb/hermes-agent/skills/<category>/<name>/SKILL.md` — committed, shipped with the package. Use `write_file` + `git add`. `skill_manage(action='create')` does NOT target this tree.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- User asks you to add a skill \"in this branch / repo / commit\"\n- You're committing a reusable workflow that should ship with hermes-agent\n- You're editing an existing skill under `/home/bb/hermes-agent/skills/` (use `patch` for small edits, `write_file` for rewrites; `skill_manage` still works for patch on in-repo skills, but not for `create`)\n\n## Required Frontmatter\n\nSource of truth: `tools/skill_manager_tool.py::_validate_frontmatter`. Hard requirements:\n\n- Starts with `---` as the first bytes (no leading blank line).\n- Closes with `\\n---\\n` before the body.\n- Parses as a YAML mapping.\n- `name` field present.\n- `description` field present, ≤ **1024 chars** (`MAX_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH`).\n- Non-empty body after the closing `---`.\n\nPeer-matched shape used by every skill under `skills/software-development/`:\n\n``​`yaml\n---\nname: my-skill-name               # lowercase, hyphens, ≤64 chars (MAX_NAME_LENGTH)\ndescription: Use when <trigger>. <one-line behavior>.\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [short, descriptive, tags]\n    related_skills: [other-skill, another-skill]\n---\n``​`\n\n`version` / `author` / `license` / `metadata` are NOT enforced by the validator, but every peer has them — omit and your skill sticks out.\n\n## Size Limits\n\n- Description: ≤ 1024 chars (enforced).\n- Full SKILL.md: ≤ 100,000 chars (enforced as `MAX_SKILL_CONTENT_CHARS`, ~36k tokens).\n- Peer skills in `software-development/` sit at **8-14k chars**. Aim for that range. If you're pushing past 20k, split into `references/*.md` and reference them from SKILL.md.\n\n## Peer-Matched Structure\n\nEvery in-repo skill follows roughly:\n\n``​`\n# <Title>\n\n## Overview\nOne or two paragraphs: what and why.\n\n## When to Use\n- Bulleted triggers\n- \"Don't use for:\" counter-triggers\n\n## <Topic sections specific to the skill>\n- Quick-reference tables are common\n- Code blocks with exact commands\n- Hermes-specific recipes (tests via scripts/run_tests.sh, ui-tui paths, etc.)\n\n## Common Pitfalls\nNumbered list of mistakes and their fixes.\n\n## Verification Checklist\n- [ ] Checkbox list of post-action verifications\n\n## One-Shot Recipes (optional)\nNamed scenarios → concrete command sequences.\n``​`\n\nNot every section is mandatory, but `Overview` + `When to Use` + actionable body + pitfalls are the minimum for the skill to feel like a peer.\n\n## Directory Placement\n\n``​`\nskills/<category>/<skill-name>/SKILL.md\n``​`\n\nCategories currently in repo (confirm with `ls skills/`): `autonomous-ai-agents`, `creative`, `data-science`, `devops`, `dogfood`, `email`, `gaming`, `github`, `leisure`, `mcp`, `media`, `mlops/*`, `note-taking`, `productivity`, `red-teaming`, `research`, `smart-home`, `social-media`, `software-development`.\n\nPick the closest existing category. Don't invent new top-level categories casually.\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. **Survey peers** in the target category:\n   ``​`\n   ls skills/<category>/\n   ``​`\n   Read 2-3 peer SKILL.md files to match tone and structure.\n2. **Check validator constraints** in `tools/skill_manager_tool.py` if unsure.\n3. **Draft** with `write_file` to `skills/<category>/<name>/SKILL.md`.\n4. **Validate locally**:\n   ``​`python\n   import yaml, re, pathlib\n   content = pathlib.Path(\"skills/<category>/<name>/SKILL.md\").read_text()\n   assert content.startswith(\"---\")\n   m = re.search(r'\\n---\\s*\\n', content[3:])\n   fm = yaml.safe_load(content[3:m.start()+3])\n   assert \"name\" in fm and \"description\" in fm\n   assert len(fm[\"description\"]) <= 1024\n   assert len(content) <= 100_000\n   ``​`\n5. **Git add + commit** on the active branch.\n6. **Note:** the CURRENT session's skill loader is cached — `skill_view` / `skills_list` will not see the new skill until a new session. This is expected, not a bug.\n\n## Cross-Referencing Other Skills\n\n`metadata.hermes.related_skills` unions both trees (`skills/` in-repo and `~/.hermes/skills/`) at load time. You CAN reference a user-local skill from an in-repo skill, but it won't resolve for other users who clone the repo fresh. Prefer referencing only in-repo skills from in-repo skills. If a frequently-referenced skill lives only in `~/.hermes/skills/`, consider promoting it to the repo.\n\n## Editing Existing In-Repo Skills\n\n- **Small fix (typo, added pitfall, tightened trigger):** `skill_manage(action='patch', name=..., old_string=..., new_string=...)` works fine on in-repo skills.\n- **Major rewrite:** `write_file` the whole SKILL.md. `skill_manage(action='edit')` also works but requires supplying the full new content.\n- **Adding supporting files:** `write_file` to `skills/<category>/<name>/references/<file>.md`, `templates/<file>`, or `scripts/<file>`. `skill_manage(action='write_file')` also works and enforces the references

{"success": true, "name": "github-repo-management", "description": "Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Repositories", "Git", "Releases", "Secrets", "Configuration"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-pr-workflow", "github-issues"], "content": "---\nname: github-repo-management\ndescription: \"Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Repositories, Git, Releases, Secrets, Configuration]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-pr-workflow, github-issues]\n---\n\n# GitHub Repository Management\n\nCreate, clone, fork, configure, and manage GitHub repositories. Each section shows `gh` first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n\n### Setup\n\n``​`bash\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if [ -f ~/.hermes/.env ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED ~/.hermes/.env; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED ~/.hermes/.env | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\([^@]*\\)@.*|\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\n\n# Get your GitHub username (needed for several operations)\nif [ \"$AUTH\" = \"gh\" ]; then\n  GH_USER=$(gh api user --jq '.login')\nelse\n  GH_USER=$(curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" https://api.github.com/user | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['login'])\")\nfi\n``​`\n\nIf you're inside a repo already:\n\n``​`bash\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## 0. Railway + GitHub: Verify Before Assuming Auto-Deploy\n\n**Never assume a Railway service is connected to GitHub.** Railway services can be deployed via CLI (`railway up`) without any GitHub connection — in that case, pushing to the repo does nothing to the running service.\n\n**Verify the running service, not just the repo.** For a production push-to-deploy claim, do a no-op commit, push it, and watch the live app for an actual change.\n\n**Quick verification pattern:**\n1. Confirm the branch is already in sync with GitHub.\n2. Create and push a harmless empty commit:\n   ``​`bash\n   git commit --allow-empty -m \"chore: trigger deploy verification\"\n   git push origin main\n   ``​`\n3. Poll the live app for a few minutes and compare a stable marker (HTML build ID, script chunk hashes, or another response detail that should change on deploy).\n4. If the push lands on GitHub but the live app stays identical, the Railway GitHub connection is not active.\n\n**Connection clues:**\n- In Railway settings, a connected service shows a repo/branch; a \"Connect Repo\" prompt means it is not wired.\n- If the service env exposes repo metadata and the owner field is empty, treat that as strong evidence the GitHub connection is missing.\n\n**Always check repo sync before connecting:**\n``​`bash\n# Check local repo status vs remote\ncd /path/to/project && git status && git log --oneline -5\n# Shows if local is ahead of origin — means GitHub may be stale\n``​`\n\n**If push is rejected, rebase first:**\n- Fetch the remote commit with token auth.\n- Rebase local `main` onto the fetched remote branch.\n- Push again.\n- See `references/github-push-rebase-fallback.md` for the exact sequence.\n\n**Setup flow when not connected:**\n1. First push all local commits to GitHub: `git push origin main`\n2. Then connect in Railway Settings → Connect Repo → select repo → branch `main`\n3. Railway will immediately deploy from current `main`, then auto-deploy on every future push\n\n**Pitfall:** if local checkout is ahead of GitHub (e.g. many commits), connecting the repo before pushing means Railway deploys stale code. Always check `git status` first.\n\nFor a repeatable verification recipe, see `references/railway-github-deploy-verification.md`.\n\n## 1. Cloning Repositories\n\nCloning is pure `git` — works identically either way:\n\nFor a quick repo/push readiness checklist in Hermes environments, see `references/repo-push-auth.md`.\nFor repo-role and location discovery in Hermes environments, see `references/local-repo-discovery.md`.\n\n``​`bash\n# Clone via HTTPS (works with credential helper or token-embedded URL)\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone into a specific directory\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git ./my-local-dir\n\n# Shallow clone (faster for large repos)\ngit clone --depth 1 https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone a specific branch\ngit clone --branch develop https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone via SSH (if SSH is configured)\ngit clone [REDACTED_EMAIL]:owner/repo-name.git\n``​`\n\n**With gh (shorthand):**\n\n``​`bash\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name -- --depth 1\n``​`\n\n## 2. Creating Repositories\n\n**With gh:**\n\n``​`bash\n# Create a public repo and clone it\ngh repo create my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# Private, with description and license\ngh repo create my-new-project --private --description \"A useful tool\" --license MIT --clone\n\n# Under an organization\ngh repo create my-org/my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# From existing local directory\ncd /path/to/existing/project\ngh repo create my-project --source . --public --push\n``​`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n``​`bash\n# Create the remote repo via API\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/user/repos \\\n  -d '{\n    \"name\": \"my-new-project\",\n    \"description\": \"A u
